This appendix keeps the detailed source-code and runtime evidence for the
Gateway G0--G18 path. The main text gives the argument and the compact
tables; this file keeps the longer audit trail so that each claim can be
checked against source and observed values.

\phantomsection
\subsection*{Appendix Reading Guide}
\label{app:reading-guide}

The appendix follows one rule: source detail belongs here, not in the main
paper. Each stage entry records its purpose, source path, R1 value, and
evidence status. The labels used below map to the main-paper observation
categories as follows.

\begin{itemize}
    \item \textsc{Runtime} / \textsc{Native Runtime}: runtime evidence
    directly supports the step; these map to \textsc{Observed}.
    \item \textsc{Observed} / \textsc{Observed(no-tool)}: compact summary
    labels used in AR tables for directly observed runtime outcomes; these
    map to \textsc{Observed}.
    \item \textsc{Derived} / \textsc{Source-Derived}: source code and
    recorded upstream values determine the branch, but the trace does not
    emit a separate event for that internal decision; these map to
    \textsc{SourceDerived}.
    \item \textsc{SourceDerived}: compact summary label equivalent to
    \textsc{Source-Derived}; this maps to \textsc{SourceDerived}.
    \item \textsc{Not Recorded}: the item is relevant but is absent from
    the saved trace; this maps to \textsc{NotObserved}.
    \item \textsc{Not Reached}: the source branch exists, but R1 did not
    execute it; this is negative evidence and maps to
    \textsc{SourceDerived} when justified by observed earlier values.
    \item \textsc{Not Applicable}: the source branch does not apply to
    R1's observed inputs; this maps to \textsc{SourceDerived}.
    \item \textsc{Observability Gap}: the stage outcome is known, but a
    lower-level predicate, field, or return payload is not emitted; the
    missing field maps to \textsc{NotObserved}.
\end{itemize}

All source anchors used for G0--G18 and the post-G18 source mapping
were mechanically checked against the unmodified upstream OpenClaw
source at commit \texttt{0790d9f} using \texttt{scripts/check\_anchors.py}.
This check validates source-anchor fidelity only. It does not
independently validate runtime evidence or the semantic correctness of
the experimental claims.

For R1, all Gateway outcomes are observed at the stage-outcome level, but
rationale visibility remains partial. The table below summarizes the
field-level gaps that appear later in the per-stage ledger.

\begin{longtable}{
    @{}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.10\linewidth}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.30\linewidth}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.52\linewidth}
    @{}
}
\textbf{Stage} & \textbf{Observed outcome} & \textbf{Rationale-level gap} \\
\hline
G0 & shared-auth state available & concrete token material and device-token candidate source are redacted or not recorded \\
G1 & shared credential allows the connection & exact credential comparison details and rate-limit internals are not emitted \\
G2 & final auth passes without device-token fallback & device-signature subpath is source-derived because \texttt{hasDeviceIdentity=False} \\
G3 & \texttt{chat.send} allowed by \texttt{operator.write} & \texttt{auth\_reason} is not emitted; admin shortcut exclusion is source-derived from scopes \\
G4 & RPC envelope validates & full raw WebSocket frame and all pre-validation fields are not archived \\
G5 & message/session fields normalize & intermediate sanitizer object is not emitted in full \\
G6 & requested Agent resolves to \texttt{main} & absence of every alternate override source is partly source-derived \\
G7 & Session key resolves & persistent store path, loaded entry object, \texttt{entry.sessionId}, and timing details are not emitted \\
G8 & Agent/Session validation passes & full validation object and backing Session identity are not emitted \\
G9 & effective Agent is \texttt{main} & fallback candidate chain is not emitted in full \\
G10 & send policy allows the turn & exact allow sub-branch and rule rationale are not emitted \\
G11 & duplicate-run check creates a new dispatch & internal lock/cache state is not emitted \\
G12 & work is admitted & admission internals, latest Session details, and concrete admitted Session identity are not emitted \\
G13 & message context is built & full constructed context object is not emitted \\
G14 & auto-reply dispatch is entered & complete dispatch return payload is not emitted \\
G15 & runtime context is finalized & full finalized context object is not emitted \\
G16 & dispatch-from-config normal path continues & complete reply-dispatch return object is not emitted \\
G17 & Agent re-check passes & fallback attempt chain is not emitted in full \\
G18 & default resolver is selected & full resolver payload and later Gateway post-return state are not emitted \\
\hline
\end{longtable}

Final \texttt{rationale\_visibility} count for G0--G18:
\textsc{full} \(=0/19\), \textsc{partial} \(=19/19\), and
\textsc{source-derived} \(=0/19\). The count is stored in
\seqsplit{artifacts/controlled/rationale-visibility-summary.csv}.

Exact page placement may change after recompilation. Therefore, the main
paper refers to appendix labels and stage names rather than fixed PDF page
numbers. This keeps the paper stable when figures, tables, or references
are edited.

\subsection*{G0: Connection Authentication State Resolution}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step converts the authentication material supplied during the
WebSocket connection handshake into a structured
\texttt{ConnectAuthState}. The Gateway receives the resolved
authentication configuration, handshake authentication fields, device
identity status, request metadata, proxy configuration, rate-limiting
state, and client IP, and passes them to
\texttt{resolveConnectAuthState(...)}. The function resolves the
available shared credential, bootstrap-token candidate, and
device-token candidate, invokes the initial shared-credential
authorization path, performs a shared-auth probe, and returns the
resulting authentication state. G0 does not necessarily make the final
connection-authentication decision. For the connection immediately
preceding R1, the shared-auth path is authorized and no explicit
device-token candidate is used. The returned state is therefore passed to
G2, where the shared-token result is accepted and the device-token
fallback is not reached.

\textit{not recorded in TraceClaw.}

\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Resolve shared-credential material}\\
\texttt{sharedConnectAuth =}\\
\quad\texttt{resolveSharedConnectAuth(connectAuth)}\\
\texttt{sharedAuthProvided = Boolean(sharedConnectAuth)}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:100--109, 136--137}\\[1mm]

The helper normalizes the shared token/password material supplied
during the connection handshake.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{sharedAuthProvided = True}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Resolve bootstrap-token candidate}\\
\texttt{bootstrapTokenCandidate =}\\
\quad\texttt{normalizeOptionalString(}\\
\quad\quad\texttt{connectAuth?.bootstrapToken}\\
\quad\texttt{)}\\
\quad\texttt{if hasDeviceIdentity else undefined}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:138--140}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{hasDeviceIdentity = False}\\
\texttt{hasBootstrapTokenCandidate = False}\\
\texttt{bootstrapTokenCandidate = None}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Resolve device-token candidate}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate, deviceTokenCandidateSource =}\\
\quad\texttt{resolveDeviceTokenCandidate(connectAuth)}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:111--124, 141--142}\\[1mm]

Inside the helper:\\
\texttt{explicitDeviceToken =}\\
\quad\texttt{normalizeOptionalString(connectAuth?.deviceToken)}\\
\texttt{if explicitDeviceToken:}\\
\quad\texttt{return explicit token with}\\
\quad\texttt{source = "explicit-device-token"}\\
\texttt{else:}\\
\quad\texttt{try connectAuth?.token as fallback}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{explicit device token is not present}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate = NOT RECORDED}\\
\texttt{explicit-device-token source = NOT REACHED}\\
\texttt{no concrete device-token candidate is archived}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Invoke initial shared-credential authorization}\\
\texttt{authResult = await}\\
\quad\texttt{authorizeWsControlUiGatewayConnect(\{...\})}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:144--153}\\[1mm]

Inputs include:\\
\texttt{resolvedAuth}\\
\texttt{sharedConnectAuth}\\
\texttt{req}\\
\texttt{trustedProxies}\\
\texttt{allowRealIpFallback}\\
\texttt{rateLimiter}\\
\texttt{clientIp}\\[1mm]

\textit{This nested authorization call is represented separately}\\
\textit{as G1 in the Gateway architecture.}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{G1 result = "allow"}\\
\texttt{authResult.ok = True}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Probe shared-auth validity}\\
Because shared authentication material exists, G0 also evaluates:\\
\texttt{sharedAuthResult =}\\
\quad\texttt{authorizeHttpGatewayConnect(\{...\})}\\
with\\
\texttt{allowTailscale = false}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:155--166}\\[1mm]

This probe determines whether the supplied shared token/password
can independently establish shared authentication.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Compute shared-auth status}\\
\texttt{sharedAuthOk =}\\
\quad\texttt{sharedAuthResult.ok}\\
\quad\texttt{and method in \{"token", "password"\}}\\
\quad\texttt{or}\\
\quad\texttt{authResult.ok}\\
\quad\texttt{and authResult.method == "trusted-proxy"}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:170--173}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{sharedAuthOk = True}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Aggregate the ConnectAuthState}\\
\texttt{return \{}\\
\quad\texttt{authResult,}\\
\quad\texttt{authOk: authResult.ok,}\\
\quad\texttt{authMethod,}\\
\quad\texttt{sharedAuthOk,}\\
\quad\texttt{sharedAuthProvided,}\\
\quad\texttt{bootstrapTokenCandidate,}\\
\quad\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate,}\\
\quad\texttt{deviceTokenCandidateSource}\\
\texttt{\}}\\
\textit{Source: auth-context.ts:175--185}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{authOk = True}\\
\texttt{authMethod = "token"}\\
\texttt{sharedAuthProvided = True}\\
\texttt{sharedAuthOk = True}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate = NOT RECORDED}\\
\texttt{explicit-device-token source = NOT REACHED}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Preserve the alternative authentication path}\\
Source code can preserve an alternative device-token path when shared
authentication fails. R1 does not use that path: shared authentication
succeeds, \texttt{hasDeviceIdentity=False}, and no concrete
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate} value is recorded in the R1 trace.\\[1mm]

R1 runtime sequence:\\
\texttt{G0 result = "authorized"}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{no device-token candidate preserved for R1}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G2 authMethod = "token"}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G2 result = "pass"}\\[2mm]

\textit{Therefore, G0 prepares authentication state;}\\
\textit{the final connection decision is completed later in G2.}
};

% ==============================================================
% G1
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G1: Initial Shared-Credential Authorization}

\textbf{Purpose:}
G1 evaluates the shared authentication credential prepared inside G0
against the Gateway's configured connection-authentication policy.
The G0 authentication-state resolver invokes
\texttt{authorizeWsControlUiGatewayConnect(...)} with the resolved
Gateway authentication configuration, normalized shared credential,
WebSocket request metadata, proxy settings, rate-limiting state, and
client IP. The wrapper marks the authentication surface as
\texttt{"ws-control-ui"} and delegates the decision to
\texttt{authorizeGatewayConnect(...)}, which eventually enters
\texttt{authorizeGatewayConnectCore(...)}. For token-mode
authentication, the core path can perform rate-limit checks and then
validate the supplied shared token against the configured Gateway token.
G1 returns a \texttt{GatewayAuthResult}; it does not validate the
device-token candidate and does not establish the final role or scopes.
For the connection immediately preceding R1, TraceClaw records
\texttt{authMode = "token"} and \texttt{result = "allow"}.
Because R1's shared token is accepted, no denial reason is expected in
the successful G1 event and no device-token fallback is needed.
G2 carries the shared-token authorization forward and finalizes role and scope.

% ==============================================================
% INTERNAL STEPS
% ==============================================================

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Enter the WS Control UI authorization wrapper}\\
\texttt{authorizeWsControlUiGatewayConnect(params)}\\
delegates to:\\
\texttt{authorizeGatewayConnect(\{}\\
\quad\texttt{...params,}\\
\quad\texttt{authSurface = "ws-control-ui"}\\
\texttt{\})}\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:597--604}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Resolve the Gateway authorization request context}\\
\texttt{resolveGatewayAuthRequestContext(params)} determines:\\
\texttt{authSurface}\\
\texttt{limiter}\\
\texttt{client IP}\\
\texttt{rateLimitScope}\\
\texttt{localDirect}\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:109--126, 456--461}\\[1mm]

For G1:\\
\texttt{authSurface = "ws-control-ui"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Select the route into the core authorization logic}\\
If the Tailscale/rate-limit serialization condition is satisfied,
\texttt{authorizeGatewayConnect(...)} invokes
\texttt{authorizeGatewayConnectCore(...)} through
\texttt{withSerializedRateLimitAttempt(...)}. Otherwise it invokes
the core function directly.\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:463--478}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\textit{The exact outer serialization branch is not recorded,}\\
\textit{but both routes enter the same core function.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Enter \texttt{authorizeGatewayConnectCore(...)}}\\
The core resolves the effective authorization context again and then
evaluates the configured authentication mode.\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:481--488}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{auth.mode = "token"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Skip unrelated authentication modes}\\
\texttt{trusted-proxy} handling is used only when\\
\texttt{auth.mode == "trusted-proxy"}.\\
\texttt{none} immediately succeeds only when\\
\texttt{auth.mode == "none"}.\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:490--535}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{mode = "token" -> both branches are not selected}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Apply the shared-secret rate-limit guard}\\
\texttt{rateLimitResult = rejectIfRateLimited(...)}\\
\texttt{if rateLimitResult exists: return it}\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:537--540}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\textit{The G1 trace does not record whether this guard was checked;}\\
\textit{the observed stage outcome is allow.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Exclude the optional Tailscale success path}\\
The Tailscale branch requires:\\
\texttt{not hasExplicitSharedSecretAuth(connectAuth)}.\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:542--560}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{sharedConnectAuth exists}\\
\texttt{sharedAuthProvided = True}\\
\texttt{-> Tailscale success is not the observed G1 result}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Enter token-mode authentication when no earlier guard returns}\\
\texttt{if auth.mode == "token":}\\
\quad\texttt{return authorizeTokenAuth(\{}\\
\quad\quad\texttt{authToken = auth.token,}\\
\quad\quad\texttt{connectToken = connectAuth?.token,}\\
\quad\quad\texttt{limiter, ip, rateLimitScope}\\
\quad\texttt{\})}\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:562--570}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 9: Token helper validates the shared token}\\
\texttt{authorizeTokenAuth(...)} can return:\\
\texttt{token\_missing\_config}\\
\texttt{token\_missing}\\
\texttt{token\_mismatch}\\
or, on success:\\
\texttt{\{ok: true, method: "token"\}}\\
\textit{Source: auth.ts:389--411}\\[1mm]

R1 runtime:\\
\texttt{G1 result = "allow"}\\
\texttt{authResult.ok = True}\\
\textit{No denial reason is emitted for the observed allow result.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 10: Return the G1 decision to G0}\\
The resulting \texttt{GatewayAuthResult} becomes G0's
\texttt{authResult}. G0 then combines it with the independently
resolved bootstrap/device-token candidates.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{G1 = allow}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G0 result = "authorized"}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{no explicit device-token candidate is present}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G2 later succeeds through the shared-token path}
};

% ==============================================================
% G2
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G2: Final Connection Authentication and Identity Finalization}

\textbf{Purpose:}
After G0 has assembled the preliminary connection-authentication state
and G1 has evaluated the shared credential, G2 performs the final
connection-authentication decision. The Gateway first validates the
presence and cryptographic consistency of the supplied device identity.
It then passes the G0 \texttt{ConnectAuthState}, device identity,
requested role and scopes, rate-limiting state, and token-verification
callbacks to \texttt{resolveConnectAuthDecision(...)}. This function
can preserve an already successful authentication result or attempt an
alternative bootstrap-token or device-token path. After authentication
succeeds, the surrounding WebSocket connection handler revalidates
Gateway-auth generation, checks device pairing and approved role/scope
access, constructs the authenticated Gateway client, and sends the
\texttt{hello-ok} response. For the WebSocket connection carrying
R1, the shared-auth path from G1 succeeds and the explicit
shared-token authorization is carried into G2.
The resulting connection uses
\texttt{authMethod = "token"}, role
\texttt{"operator"}, and scope \texttt{operator.write}.
TraceClaw records \texttt{result = "pass"}.

% ==============================================================
% INTERNAL STEPS
% ==============================================================

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Check device-identity requirement}\\
The Gateway first evaluates whether the connection is allowed to proceed
without a device identity.\\
\texttt{evaluateMissingDeviceIdentity(...)}\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:1038--1136}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{hasDeviceIdentity = False}\\
\texttt{-> missing-device rejection is not taken}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Validate device identity and signed handshake data}\\
The Gateway derives the device ID from the public key and checks:\\
\texttt{derived device ID == claimed device ID}\\
\texttt{signature timestamp is fresh}\\
\texttt{nonce exists and matches the connection nonce}\\
\texttt{signature payload version is valid}\\
\texttt{public key can be normalized}\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:1137--1213}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{hasDeviceIdentity = False}\\
\texttt{device-identity checks are NOT APPLICABLE}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Enter the final authentication decision}\\
\texttt{resolveConnectAuthDecision(...)} receives the G0 authentication
state together with the validated device identity, role, scopes,
rate-limiter state, and token-verification callbacks.\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:1215 onward;}\\
\textit{auth-context.ts:188 onward}\\[1mm]

R1 input:\\
\texttt{authOk = True}\\
\texttt{bootstrapTokenCandidate = None}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate = NOT RECORDED}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Skip bootstrap-token authentication}\\
The bootstrap-token path is evaluated only when a bootstrap token,
device ID, public key, and device identity are all available.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{bootstrapTokenCandidate = None}\\
\texttt{-> bootstrap-token branch is not taken}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Determine whether device-token fallback is required}\\
The function returns early if authentication has already succeeded,
there is no device identity, there is no device ID, or no device-token
candidate exists.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{authOk = True}\\
\texttt{hasDeviceIdentity = False}\\
\texttt{deviceId = NOT APPLICABLE}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenCandidate = NOT RECORDED}\\
\texttt{-> device-token verification is not reached}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Apply the device-token rate-limit guard}\\
If a rate limiter is configured, the Gateway checks the device-token
authentication scope before verifying the token. A denied rate-limit
check would stop authentication.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{device-token rate-limit guard is not reached}\\
\textit{Exact limiter presence is not recorded by TraceClaw.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Verify the explicit device token}\\
\texttt{tokenCheck = await verifyDeviceToken(\{}\\
\quad\texttt{deviceId, deviceTokenCandidate, role, scopes}\\
\texttt{\})}\\[1mm]

On success:\\
\texttt{authOk = True}\\
\texttt{authMethod = "device-token"}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{branch status = NOT REACHED}\\
\texttt{tokenCheck.ok = NOT RECORDED / NOT APPLICABLE}\\
\texttt{device-token success is not attributed to R1}\\[1mm]

\textit{R1 records sharedAuthProvided=True, sharedAuthOk=True,}\\
\textit{and hasDeviceIdentity=False; authMethod="token" therefore}\\
\textit{must not be read as proof of device-token authentication.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Return the final ConnectAuthDecision}\\
The function returns:\\
\texttt{authResult}\\
\texttt{authOk}\\
\texttt{authMethod}\\
\texttt{deviceTokenSharedGatewaySessionGeneration}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{authOk = True}\\
\texttt{authMethod = "token"}\\
\texttt{issuer generation = not directly observed}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 9: Accept the authentication decision}\\
The outer WebSocket handler copies the returned authentication state.
If \texttt{authOk} is false, it rejects the connection; otherwise the
handshake advances to the authenticated state.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{authOk = True -> reject branch not taken}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 10: Revalidate authentication generation}\\
For connection modes linked to shared Gateway authentication, the
Gateway checks whether the credential generation has changed while the
handshake was in progress. A stale generation closes the connection.\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{no generation-mismatch close occurs}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 11: Validate device pairing, role, and scopes}\\
The Gateway obtains the paired-device state and verifies that the
device is allowed to use the requested role and scopes. Missing or
upgraded access can trigger the pairing / approval path.\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:1335--1757}\\[1mm]

R1 finalized access:\\
\texttt{role = "operator"}\\
\texttt{scopes = [operator.write]}\\

\textit{The exact internal pairing sub-branch is not directly}\\
\textit{identified by the G2 runtime event.}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 12: Device-token state maintenance is not used by R1}\\
For a server-approved device baseline, the Gateway may call
\texttt{ensureDeviceToken(...)} using the finalized device ID,
role, and scopes.\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:1759--1813}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 13: Register the authenticated Gateway client}\\
The Gateway constructs the authenticated \texttt{GatewayWsClient},
records whether the connection used device-token authentication; R1 does not use it,
registers it with \texttt{setClient(...)}, and marks the handshake
as connected.\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:2056--2065}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{isDeviceTokenAuth = False}\\
\texttt{SOURCE-DERIVED from shared-token path /}\\
\texttt{not-reached device-token fallback}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 14: Build and send the authenticated hello response}\\
The \texttt{hello-ok} payload includes the finalized role,
authenticated scopes and server capabilities; device-token information is not applicable to R1
when applicable. The response is sent and the handshake advances to
the ready state.\\
\textit{Source: message-handler.ts:2169--2204, 2237--2273}\\[1mm]

R1 runtime:\\
\texttt{event = "connection\_identity\_finalized"}\\
\texttt{result = "pass"}\\
\texttt{authMode = "token"}\\
\texttt{authMethod = "token"}\\
\texttt{role = "operator"}\\
\texttt{clientMode = "webchat"}\\
\texttt{scopes = [operator.write]}\\[3mm]

\textbf{G2 conclusion:}\\
\textit{The WebSocket connection carrying R1 is now fully}\\
\textit{authenticated and ready. The next concrete R1 RPC step}\\
\textit{is G3 method authorization for \texttt{chat.send}.}
};

\end{center}

\subsection*{G3: Method Authorization}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step performs method-level authorization after the Gateway connection
has been authenticated. It determines whether the authenticated client may
invoke \texttt{chat.send} from its connection role and scopes. It does not
validate the \texttt{chat.send} request body or inspect the semantic content
of the user message. In R1, the authenticated client has role
\texttt{operator} and includes the \texttt{operator.write} scope. The
administrator shortcut is not taken because \texttt{operator.admin} is
absent, so the ordinary \texttt{operator.write} required-scope check is
reached.

\subsection*{G3: Method Authorization Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step performs method-level authorization for the authenticated
Gateway client. It checks whether the current operator is allowed to
invoke \texttt{chat.send}. In R1, the authenticated connection
contains \texttt{operator.write}; the administrator shortcut is not
taken, so the normal \texttt{operator.write} scope-checking branch is
reached.

\texttt{)}\\[3mm]

Executed helper functions:\\[1mm]

\texttt{parseGatewayRole(...)}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{isRoleAuthorizedForMethod(...)}\\[3mm]

Then inline authorization branches:\\
\texttt{admin shortcut check}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{required-scope check}\\
$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{return null}
};

\quad\texttt{scopes = ["operator.write"]}\\[2mm]

};

\quad\texttt{= False}\\[1mm]

\quad\texttt{= False}\\[1mm]

\quad\texttt{\_reached = True}
};

% ==============================================================
% Internal steps
% ==============================================================

\node[stepbox, below=30mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[2mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Check pre-connect shortcut}\\
\texttt{if client.connect is absent:}\\
\quad\texttt{return None}\\
\textit{Source: lines 270--272}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{client.connect exists -> continue}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Check health-method shortcut}\\
\texttt{if method == "health":}\\
\quad\texttt{return None}\\
\textit{Source: lines 273--275}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{method = "chat.send" -> continue}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Resolve role}\\
\texttt{role\_raw = client.connect.role or "operator"}\\
\texttt{role = parseGatewayRole(role\_raw)}\\
\texttt{if role is invalid: reject request}\\
\textit{Source: lines 276--280}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{role = "operator"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Check role authorization}\\
\texttt{scopes = client.connect.scopes or []}\\
\texttt{if not isRoleAuthorizedForMethod(role, method):}\\
\quad\texttt{reject request}\\
\textit{Source: lines 281--284}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{operator is authorized for chat.send -> continue}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Check node shortcut}\\
\texttt{if role == "node":}\\
\quad\texttt{return None}\\
\textit{Source: lines 285--287}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{role != "node" -> continue}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Check admin shortcut}\\
\texttt{if "operator.admin" in scopes:}\\
\quad\texttt{return None}\\
\textit{Source: lines 288--290}\\[1mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{"operator.admin" is not present}\\
\texttt{-> admin shortcut not taken}\\
\texttt{-> continue to required-scope check}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Perform required-scope authorization}\\
\texttt{registered\_scope = methodRegistry.getScope(method)}\\
\texttt{scope\_auth =}\\
\quad\texttt{authorizeOperatorScopesForRequiredScope(...)}\\
\quad\texttt{or authorizeOperatorScopesForMethod(...)}\\
\texttt{if not scope\_auth.allowed: reject request}\\
\textit{Source: lines 291--297}\\[1mm]

For \texttt{chat.send}:\\
\texttt{registered\_scope = "operator.write"}\\[1mm]

\textbf{R1: Step 7 is reached because the admin shortcut is not taken.}
};

\subsection*{G4: \texttt{chat.send} Request Validation}

This step validates the incoming \texttt{chat.send} RPC envelope before
its contents are processed further. It validates the request against

normalizes any explicit originating route, and rejects protected
provenance or routing fields when the caller lacks administrator scope.

administrator scope. For R1, the request conforms to the schema,
contains no privileged metadata or explicit origin, and therefore

content, identifies control requests such as \texttt{/stop} or

Agent override, and per-request run identifier needed by the following
routing stages.

\subsection*{G5: Message Sanitization and Classification Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step converts the validated \texttt{chat.send} request into the
normalized message representation used by the subsequent routing stages.
It sanitizes the message text, validates optional system provenance
metadata, detects control requests such as \texttt{/stop} and
\texttt{/btw}, normalizes attachments, rejects an empty message with no
attachments, and extracts the existing Session key, optional Agent
override, and per-request run identifier. For R1, the message
\texttt{"How to make a cake?"} is unchanged by sanitization, is classified
as a normal \texttt{main} turn, contains no attachments, and proceeds
with the existing Session key and run ID.

\subsection*{G6: Requested Agent Resolution}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step determines whether the current request itself explicitly
selects an Agent. An explicit \texttt{agentId} takes precedence. If no
explicit Agent is supplied, the session key is parsed, but the parsed
Agent is returned at this stage only when the session resolves to the
special \texttt{global} store. For R1, the Dashboard session is not
a global-store request, so \texttt{requestedAgentId} remains undefined.

% G6

\subsection*{G6: Requested Agent Resolution Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step determines whether the current request explicitly selects an
Agent for the subsequent Session lookup. An explicit \texttt{agentId}
takes precedence. If no explicit Agent is supplied, the Session key is
parsed and its embedded Agent identifier is considered only when the
request resolves to the special \texttt{global} Session store. For
R1, no explicit Agent override is supplied. Although the Dashboard
Session key contains the Agent identifier \texttt{main}, it does not
resolve to the \texttt{global} store at this stage. Therefore,
\texttt{requestedAgentId} remains undefined and no Agent-specific
Session-load option is created.

\subsection*{G7: Session Resolution and Store Lookup}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step resolves the supplied session key. The Gateway measures the
session-resolution operation, invokes \texttt{loadSessionEntry()},
resolves the appropriate session store, searches the candidate store keys,
and returns the canonical key plus any loaded session record. R1 directly
records the canonical Session key and the G7 \texttt{resolved} outcome.
It does not directly record the store path, the loaded \texttt{entry}
object, or \texttt{entry.sessionId}.

\subsection*{G7: Session Resolution and Store Lookup Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step resolves the supplied Session key. The Gateway invokes
\texttt{loadSessionEntry()} inside a diagnostics and timing wrapper. The
function obtains the current runtime configuration, canonicalizes the
supplied key, resolves the appropriate Agent-owned Session store, and
searches candidate store keys. For R1, the trace records that G7 resolves
and that the canonical key remains unchanged. It does not directly record
the store path, the loaded \texttt{entry} object, \texttt{entry.sessionId},
or the measured Session-load duration.

\subsection*{G8: Agent / Session Consistency Validation}

\textbf{Purpose:}

This step validates whether an explicitly requested Agent is compatible
with the current Session routing, computes a backing Session identity
when one is available, and rejects Sessions whose owning Agent has been
removed from the current configuration. For R1, no explicit Agent
override was supplied, so Agent-selection validation succeeds
immediately. The trace records the G8 \texttt{pass} outcome. It does not
directly emit the loaded \texttt{SessionEntry} or the concrete backing
Session ID, so those fields remain \texttt{NOT RECORDED}.

% ==============================================================
% G9
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G9: Effective Agent Resolution}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step resolves the effective Gateway-side Agent for the current
Session after the Agent / Session consistency checks in G8 have passed.
The Gateway invokes \texttt{resolveSessionAgentId()} with the canonical
Session key, current runtime configuration, and any Agent identifier
validated by G8. The underlying resolver applies the precedence
explicit Agent $\rightarrow$ Session-key Agent $\rightarrow$ fallback
Agent $\rightarrow$ default Agent. For R1, no explicit Agent survives
G8, while the canonical Session key encodes \texttt{main}. Therefore,
the Session-key Agent branch determines the effective Agent as
\texttt{main}. The semantic content of
\texttt{"How to make a cake?"} is not used for this routing decision.

    @{}
}

% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G9: Effective Agent Resolution Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step resolves the effective Gateway-side Agent after the
Agent / Session consistency checks in G8 have passed. The Gateway
calls \texttt{resolveSessionAgentId()} using the canonical Session key,
the current runtime configuration, and the optional Agent identifier
returned by G8. The underlying resolver applies the precedence
explicit Agent $\rightarrow$ Session-key Agent $\rightarrow$ fallback
Agent $\rightarrow$ default Agent. For R1, no explicit Agent is
supplied, while the canonical Session key encodes \texttt{main}.
Therefore, the Session-key Agent branch is selected and the effective
Agent is resolved as \texttt{main}.

\subsection*{G10: Session Send-Policy Evaluation}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step evaluates whether the resolved Session is permitted to
continue through the \texttt{chat.send} path. The Gateway calls
\texttt{resolveSendPolicy()} with the current runtime configuration,
resolved SessionEntry, canonical Session key, and Session channel /
chat-type metadata. The policy resolver first honors a SessionEntry
override, then evaluates the configured Session send policy, including
ambiguous-key protection and rule matching. A matching deny decision
terminates the request immediately. For R1, the observed policy
decision is \texttt{allow}, so the deny gate is not taken and execution
continues toward G11. The runtime trace does not identify which internal
allow-producing branch generated the final decision.

continue through the \texttt{chat.send} path. The Gateway evaluates

the deny gate is not taken and execution continues to G11. The
available trace does not identify which internal allow-producing
branch generated the final decision.

\texttt{"allow"} or \texttt{"deny"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Load configured Session send policy}\\
\texttt{policy = cfg.session?.sendPolicy}\\
\texttt{if policy is absent: return "allow"}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:88--91}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Reject ambiguous legacy peer-key shape}\\
\texttt{if hasAmbiguousPeerShape(sessionKey):}\\
\quad\texttt{return "deny"}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:92--96}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Normalize Session routing metadata}\\
\texttt{raw\_session\_key = sessionKey or ""}\\
\texttt{stripped\_session\_key =}\\
\quad\texttt{stripAgentSessionKeyPrefix(raw\_session\_key) or ""}\\
\texttt{normalize raw and stripped Session keys}\\
\texttt{resolve channel lazily from}\\
\quad\texttt{params.channel / entry.channel /}\\
\quad\texttt{entry.lastChannel / Session key}\\
\texttt{resolve chat type from}\\
\quad\texttt{params.chatType / entry.chatType / Session key}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:98--117}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Evaluate configured policy rules}\\
\texttt{for rule in policy.rules or []:}\\
\quad\texttt{normalize action and match constraints}\\
\quad\texttt{check channel}\\
\quad\texttt{check chat type}\\
\quad\texttt{check rawKeyPrefix}\\
\quad\texttt{check keyPrefix}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:119--146}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Apply matched rule decision}\\
\texttt{if matching action == "deny":}\\
\quad\texttt{return "deny"}\\
\texttt{otherwise mark allowed\_match = True}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:147--151}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Return allow after an allow-rule match}\\
\texttt{if allowed\_match:}\\
\quad\texttt{return "allow"}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:153--155}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Apply policy default / final fallback}\\
\texttt{fallback = normalizeSendPolicy(policy.default)}\\
\texttt{return fallback or "allow"}\\
\textit{Source: send-policy.ts:157--158}\\[2mm]

\textbf{R1 observed execution:}\\
\texttt{send\_policy = "allow"}\\
\texttt{deny\_gate\_taken = False}\\
\texttt{result = "allow"}\\[1mm]

\textit{Observability gap: the G10 runtime event records the final}\\
\textit{policy decision, but does not identify which internal}\\
\textit{allow-producing branch returned the value.}
};

% ==============================================================
% G11
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G11: Idempotency and Existing-Run Guard}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step determines whether the incoming \texttt{chat.send} request
represents a genuinely new dispatch or a retry / duplicate of an
already completed, aborted, pending, active, or queued run. The
Gateway checks multiple run-state registries using the client-provided
run ID and short-circuits when an existing state is found. Only a
request that passes all existing-run guards is allowed to proceed as
a new dispatch. Before entering Session lifecycle admission, the
Gateway also checks whether Session routing has changed and whether
the resolved Session is permitted to start new work. For R1,
TraceClaw records \texttt{dedupeDecision = "new\_dispatch"} and
\texttt{result = "pass"}, so no existing-run short-circuit handles
the request and execution continues toward G12.

% ==============================================================
% G11
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G11: Idempotency and Existing-Run Guard Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step determines whether the incoming \texttt{chat.send} request
is a genuinely new dispatch or a retry / duplicate of an existing run.
The Gateway checks completed, aborted, pending, active, and queued run
state using the request run ID. Any existing state short-circuits the
request. A request that survives these guards is treated as a new
dispatch and is additionally checked for Session-routing consistency
and Session work eligibility before lifecycle admission. For R1,
TraceClaw records \texttt{dedupeDecision = "new\_dispatch"} and
\texttt{result = "pass"}, so execution continues toward G12.

% ==============================================================
% G12
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G12: Session Work Admission and Revalidation}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step converts the new-dispatch candidate from G11 into an admitted
Session-scoped run. The Gateway first captures the current Agent
lifecycle generation and creates a temporary pending reservation. It then
invokes \texttt{beginSessionWorkAdmission(...)} using the resolved
Session key and any available backing identity. While acquiring the
admission lease, the Gateway revalidates reservation ownership,
lifecycle generation, reservation expiry, latest Session state, routing
consistency, backing Session identity when present, and archival status.
If these checks succeed, an active chat AbortController is registered and
the run is admitted. Post-admission race checks are then performed before
the Agent run context is claimed. For R1, TraceClaw records
\texttt{admissionDecision = "admitted"},
\texttt{result = "pass"}, and
\texttt{reason = "latest\_session\_revalidated"}.

% ==============================================================
% G12
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G12: Session Work Admission and Revalidation Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step converts the new-dispatch candidate into an admitted
Session-scoped run. The Gateway creates a temporary run reservation,
acquires a Session work-admission lease, revalidates the latest
Session and lifecycle state, registers the active run AbortController,
performs post-admission race checks, and finally claims the Agent run
context. For R1, the latest Session remains consistent with the
previously resolved Session and TraceClaw records
\texttt{admissionDecision = "admitted"},
\texttt{result = "pass"}, and
\texttt{reason = "latest\_session\_revalidated"}.

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Capture Agent lifecycle generation}\\
\texttt{lifecycleGeneration = getAgentEventLifecycleGeneration()}\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4025; agent-events.ts:195--197}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Create a temporary pending-run reservation}\\
\texttt{pendingAttemptId = randomUUID()}\\
\texttt{pendingExpiresAtMs = resolveChatRunExpiresAtMs(...)}\\
\texttt{context.dedupe.set(pendingChatSendKey, \{...\})}\\
Reservation records the run ID, attempt ID, Session key,
owner connection/device, expiry, and turn kind.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4026--4047}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Request Session-scoped work admission}\\
\texttt{beginSessionWorkAdmission(\{}\\
\quad\texttt{scope: storePath,}\\
\quad\texttt{identities: [sessionKey, backingSessionId],}\\
\quad\texttt{assertAllowed: ...,}\\
\quad\texttt{onInterrupt: ...}\\
\texttt{\})}\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4067--4163;}\\
\textit{helper: session-lifecycle-admission.ts:327--378}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Revalidate reservation ownership}\\
Inside \texttt{assertAllowed}:\\
\texttt{check chatAbortedRuns}\\
\texttt{readPreRegisteredRun(pendingChatSendKey)}\\
\texttt{verify attemptId == pendingAttemptId}\\
\texttt{detect terminal / active superseding run}\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4070--4093}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Revalidate lifecycle generation and expiry}\\
\texttt{if lifecycleGeneration changed: abort as "restart"}\\
\texttt{if reservation missing or expired: abort as "timeout"}\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4094--4116}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Reload the latest persistent Session}\\
\texttt{latestSession = loadSessionEntry(rawSessionKey, sessionLoadOptions)}\\
This deliberately re-reads Session state rather than relying only on
the earlier G7 snapshot.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4117}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Revalidate Session routing and identity}\\
\texttt{sessionRoutingChanged(latestSession.cfg)}\\
\texttt{latestEntry = latestSession.entry}\\
Reject if the Session disappeared.\\
Reject if \texttt{latestEntry.sessionId != backingSessionId}.\\
Re-run \texttt{resolveSessionWorkStartError(...)}.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4118--4138}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{revalidation passes; reason = latest\_session\_revalidated}\\
\texttt{sessionId = "NOT RECORDED"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Register the admitted active run}\\
\texttt{admittedRunAbort = registerChatAbortController(...)}\\
The registration binds the run to the admitted Session identity,
timeout, provider information, turn type, and lifecycle generation.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4139--4155}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 9: Perform post-admission race checks}\\
After clearing the pending reservation:\\
\texttt{check reservationSuperseded}\\
\texttt{check lifecycle generation again}\\
\texttt{check activeRunAbort exists}\\
\texttt{check activeRunAbort.registered}\\
Any failure short-circuits instead of creating duplicate work.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4173--4235}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 10: Claim the Agent run context}\\
\texttt{claimAgentRunContext(clientRunId, \{}\\
\quad\texttt{sessionKey, admittedSessionId, lifecycleGeneration}\\
\texttt{\})}\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4240--4244}\\[2mm]

R1 runtime:\\
\texttt{admissionDecision = "admitted"}\\
\texttt{result = "pass"}\\
\texttt{reason = "latest\_session\_revalidated"}
};

% ==============================================================
% G13
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G13: Runtime Message Context Construction}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step converts the message, Session identity, Agent identity,
routing metadata, command state, and authenticated Gateway-client
state prepared by the preceding stages into a concrete
\texttt{MsgContext}. The Gateway first derives the command-facing and
Agent-facing forms of the message, extracts the originating delivery
route, and then constructs \texttt{ctx} inline. The resulting context
contains the canonical Session key, effective Agent ID, internal
message channel, command-turn state, run identity, and Gateway client
scopes. Optional staged-media fields are added only when media
offloads exist, and managed-media fields are merged immediately before
dispatch. G13 does not perform a new Session lookup, Agent selection,
send-policy decision, or model invocation. For R1, TraceClaw records
\texttt{event = "runtime\_context\_constructed"},
\texttt{AgentId = "main"}, scope \texttt{operator.write}, and
\texttt{result = "constructed"}.

\subsection*{G13: Runtime Message Context Construction Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step packages the prepared message, canonical Session identity,
effective Agent identity, routing metadata, command state, run identity,
authenticated client scopes, and optional media metadata into the
\texttt{MsgContext} consumed by the downstream G14 dispatch path.
For R1, the resulting context retains
\texttt{"How to make a cake?"}, the existing canonical Session key,
and \texttt{AgentId = "main"}. TraceClaw records the runtime context
as successfully constructed.

% ==============================================================
% INTERNAL STEPS
% ==============================================================

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Prepare command-facing message state}\\
Trim the parsed message and preserve the original message as the
command-facing body. A \texttt{commandSource} is assigned only when
command interpretation is enabled and the trimmed message begins with
\texttt{/}.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4475--4478}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{parsedMessage = "How to make a cake?"}\\
$\rightarrow$
\texttt{commandBody = "How to make a cake?"}\\
$\rightarrow$
\texttt{commandSource = undefined}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Prepare the Agent-facing message}\\
Construct \texttt{messageForAgent}. If a system-provenance receipt is
present, prepend it to the parsed message; otherwise preserve the
original message text.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4479--4481}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{messageForAgent = "How to make a cake?"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Extract originating routing metadata}\\
Project the already-resolved routing state into the fields required by
the runtime context, including \texttt{originatingChannel},
\texttt{originatingTo}, \texttt{accountId},
\texttt{messageThreadId}, and \texttt{explicitDeliverRoute}.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4489--4495}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Construct the core \texttt{MsgContext}}\\
Create \texttt{ctx: MsgContext} and populate the prepared message,
input provenance, canonical Session identity, effective Agent identity,
internal Provider / Surface, routing metadata, and direct-chat state.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4503--4519}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{Body = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{BodyForAgent = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{BodyForCommands = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{RawBody = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{SessionKey = existing canonical key}\\
\texttt{AgentId = "main"}\\
\texttt{Provider = INTERNAL\_MESSAGE\_CHANNEL}\\
\texttt{Surface = INTERNAL\_MESSAGE\_CHANNEL}\\
\texttt{ChatType = "direct"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Construct command authorization and turn state}\\
Set the context-level command-interpretation state and classify the
current message as either a text slash-command turn or an ordinary
message turn.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4520--4534}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{CommandAuthorized = true}\\
\texttt{CommandTurn.kind = "normal"}\\
\texttt{CommandTurn.source = "message"}\\
\texttt{CommandTurn.authorized = false}\\[2mm]

These two authorization values describe different levels:
command interpretation is enabled for the context, while this specific
message is not itself an authorized slash-command turn.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Attach run identity and authenticated client state}\\
Bind the current client run to the message through
\texttt{MessageSid}. For non-Operator-UI callers, sender identity may
also be attached. Finally propagate the authenticated Gateway client
scopes into the context.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4535--4545}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{MessageSid =}\\
\texttt{"ANON-R1-RUN-ID"}\\[1mm]

\texttt{GatewayClientScopes =}\\
\texttt{[operator.write]}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Attach staged media when present}\\
If staged media paths exist, extend the same \texttt{MsgContext} with
\texttt{MediaPath}, \texttt{MediaPaths}, media types,
\texttt{MediaWorkspaceDir}, and the \texttt{MediaStaged} marker.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4548--4559}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{hasAttachments = false}\\
$\rightarrow$ no staged-media fields are added.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Merge managed-media fields before dispatch}\\
Immediately before the dispatch boundary, merge any managed-media
fields into the already-constructed \texttt{MsgContext}. This keeps
managed media on the same context object that is later passed into G14.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:1482--1515, 4637}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
No managed-media state changes the context.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 9: Hand the completed context to G14}\\
The completed runtime context becomes the \texttt{ctx} field of the
G14 \texttt{dispatchInboundMessage(...)} call.\\
\textit{Source: chat.ts:4788--4797}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G13 OUTPUT: ctx: MsgContext}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G14 INPUT: params.ctx}\\[3mm]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
O_{13}
=
I_{14}.\texttt{ctx}
}
\]

R1 TraceClaw:\\
\texttt{event = "runtime\_context\_constructed"}\\
\texttt{agentId = "main"}\\
\texttt{result = "constructed"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Stage boundary}\\[2mm]

G13 ends after materializing and enriching the Gateway
\texttt{MsgContext}. It does not yet construct the model system prompt,
load conversation history, retrieve memory, resolve skills / tools, or
invoke the model. Those operations belong to deeper downstream runtime
processing.
};

% ==============================================================
% G14
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G14: Inbound Message Dispatch}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step transfers the Gateway-constructed \texttt{MsgContext} into
OpenClaw's auto-reply dispatch pipeline. The Gateway executes
\texttt{dispatchInboundMessage(...)} inside the admitted
Session-work region and the native
\texttt{gateway.chat\_send.dispatch\_inbound} diagnostic span.
The dispatch function propagates any runtime tool restriction,
initializes and binds downstream reply-run state, invokes G15 to
finalize the inbound context, records optional message-received
diagnostics, installs reply-payload delivery hooks, and invokes G16
through \texttt{dispatchReplyFromConfig(...)}. When downstream dispatch
returns, G14 reconciles the result with dispatcher cancellation and
failure counts. G15 and G16 are nested operations inside G14 rather
than independent parallel stages. For R1, the native Gateway
diagnostic span records the same run ID and canonical Session key,
\texttt{AgentId = "main"}, \texttt{provider = "openai"},
\texttt{model = "gpt-5.5"}, and \texttt{hasAttachments = false}.

\subsection*{G14: Inbound Message Dispatch Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step receives the \texttt{MsgContext} prepared by G13 and sends it
into the auto-reply pipeline. G14 prepares the reply runtime state,
finalizes the inbound context through G15, invokes G16 for reply
dispatch, and reconciles the returned result before producing
\texttt{DispatchInboundResult}.

\textbf{Step 1: Enter G14 with the G13 context}\\

\textit{Source: chat.ts:4788--4797; dispatch.ts:528--583}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{Body = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{AgentId = "main"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Prepare reply options and run state}\\
Apply an optional runtime \texttt{toolsAllow} restriction, initialize
the reply-payload run state when needed, and bind it to the downstream
reply options.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch.ts:69--80; 410--422}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Finalize the inbound context through G15}\\
Call \texttt{finalizeInboundContext(params.ctx)} to convert the incoming
context into the canonical \texttt{FinalizedMsgContext} used by the
reply pipeline.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch.ts:544--551}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G14 params.ctx}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\textbf{G15}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{finalized: FinalizedMsgContext}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Prepare diagnostics and reply delivery}\\
If diagnostics are enabled, record the finalized message metadata.
Then install the reply-payload hook before downstream reply delivery.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch.ts:552--561}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Dispatch the finalized context through G16}\\
Pass the finalized context, configuration, dispatcher, reply options,
and optional resolver into \texttt{dispatchReplyFromConfig(...)}.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch.ts:562--582}\\[2mm]

\texttt{finalized}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\textbf{G16}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{DispatchFromConfigResult}\\[2mm]

G16 is nested inside G14; G17 and G18 occur further inside G16.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Reconcile and return the dispatch result}\\
After G16 returns, G14 removes cancelled or failed queued payloads from
the public counts, updates \texttt{queuedFinal}, and includes
\texttt{failedCounts} only when failures are present. The normalized
result is then returned as \texttt{DispatchInboundResult}.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch.ts:488--524; return call at 583}\\[3mm]

\texttt{G16 result}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{finalizeDispatchResult(...)}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G14 OUTPUT: DispatchInboundResult}\\[4mm]

\textbf{Stage relationship}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G13 output ctx}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\textbf{G14}\\
\quad\texttt{\small G15 and G16 execute inside G14}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{DispatchInboundResult}
};

% G15

\subsection*{G15: Inbound Context Finalization}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step canonicalizes the \texttt{MsgContext} created by G13 before
it enters configuration-aware reply dispatch. G15 is called
synchronously inside G14 through
\texttt{finalizeInboundContext(...)} and is wrapped by the native
diagnostic span \texttt{auto\_reply.finalize\_context}. The function
projects supplemental context, normalizes inbound text and system-tag
content, canonicalizes \texttt{ChatType}, resolves the effective
Agent-facing and command-facing message bodies, derives the
conversation label, canonicalizes command authorization and command
turn state, and aligns media-type metadata when media is present.
G15 does not perform a new Session lookup, Agent selection, model
selection, memory retrieval, skill loading, tool execution, or model
inference. Its output is a \texttt{FinalizedMsgContext} consumed by
G16. For R1, the ordinary direct text message and the previously
resolved Session / Agent identity remain stable through this
deterministic normalization path.

% ==============================================================
% G16

\subsection*{G16: Reply Dispatch Orchestration}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step is the main configuration-aware reply-dispatch orchestration
stage. G16 receives the \texttt{FinalizedMsgContext} produced by G15
and prepares the complete downstream reply environment before and
after the Agent-runtime handoff.

The function first checks whether dispatch has already been aborted,
derives diagnostic and Session-lifecycle state, resolves the relevant
Session-store entry, and determines the effective dispatch Session,
including any ACP-bound Session. It then re-resolves the effective
downstream Agent in G17, establishes reply routing and runtime state,
and acquires reply-operation lifecycle ownership.

Before the default Agent reply resolver is reached, G16 loads runtime
plugins, builds hook and inbound-claim contexts, conditionally stages
remote media for hook consumers, resolves reply routing, evaluates the
downstream send policy and effective tool policy, determines
source-reply visibility, and performs physical inbound-message dedupe.
Plugin-owned claims, fast-abort handling, \texttt{before\_dispatch}
hooks, and \texttt{reply\_dispatch} hooks may terminate the path before
the default Agent runtime is entered.

If none of those paths handles the message, G16 acquires final dispatch
ownership and prepares the callback / delivery contract. It then
reaches G18, where the supplied or default reply resolver is selected
and the message is handed to the deeper reply / Agent runtime.
After G18 returns, G16 resumes control, applies Session metadata
changes, processes final reply payloads, performs delivery and dedupe
bookkeeping, completes the diagnostic lifecycle and reply operation,
and returns a \texttt{DispatchFromConfigResult}.

For R1, there is no separate G16-specific TraceClaw result event.
However, the same execution produces the G17 event
\texttt{effective\_agent\_reresolved} with
\texttt{agentId = "main"} and
\texttt{downstreamAgentId = "main"}, followed by the G18 event
\texttt{reply\_resolver\_selected} with
\texttt{resolverSource = "default\_getReplyFromConfig"}. These runtime
observations show that R1 traverses the normal G16 path through
effective-Agent re-resolution and reaches the default reply-resolver
boundary.

\subsection*{G16: Reply Dispatch Orchestration Flow Diagram}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step receives the finalized context from G15 and carries out the
configuration-aware reply-dispatch flow. G16 resolves the downstream
Session and Agent, establishes reply execution state, applies routing
and policy checks, handles deduplication and interception paths, invokes
the reply resolver through G18, and returns the resulting
\texttt{DispatchFromConfigResult} to G14.

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Receive the finalized context from G15}\\
G16 starts from \texttt{dispatchReplyFromConfig(params)}. The
\texttt{ctx} field is the \texttt{FinalizedMsgContext} produced by G15,
together with configuration, dispatcher state, reply options, and
optional callbacks.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:1215--1228}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
\texttt{Body = "How to make a cake?"}\\
\texttt{AgentId = "main"}\\
\texttt{ChatType = "direct"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Resolve the downstream Session and Agent}\\
Resolve the Session state used for the downstream dispatch path,
including any applicable bound Session state. The effective Agent is
then resolved again from this downstream Session context.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:1341--1464}\\[2mm]

The Agent re-resolution is the nested G17 stage.\\[2mm]

R1 runtime observation:\\
\texttt{agentId = "main"}\\
$\rightarrow$
\texttt{downstreamAgentId = "main"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Establish reply execution ownership}\\
Set up the reply-operation lifecycle and determine whether this message
can continue into the active reply path. Existing, stale, cancelled, or
otherwise conflicting execution state may cause an early return or
recovery path.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:1521--1623}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Prepare routing and policy state}\\
Build the downstream reply environment, including effective reply
routing and the Send, Tool, and reply-visibility policies used later in
the dispatch path. Plugin and hook context is also prepared in this
region.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:1877--2321}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Apply deduplication and interception checks}\\
Check whether the inbound message has already been claimed or handled.
G16 also evaluates fast-abort and plugin interception paths before
entering the normal reply-resolver flow.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:2342--3031}\\[2mm]

If one of these paths handles the request, G16 does not need to invoke
the normal resolver path.\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 6: Invoke the reply resolver through G18}\\
After the dispatch checks pass, select the configured
\texttt{replyResolver}. If no custom resolver is supplied, the default
path uses \texttt{getReplyFromConfig}.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3033--3811}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G16 dispatch state}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\textbf{G18}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{reply resolver result}\\[2mm]

R1 runtime observation:\\
\texttt{resolverSource =}\\
\texttt{"default\_getReplyFromConfig"}\\
\texttt{result = "selected"}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 7: Process and deliver resolver output}\\
After the resolver returns, G16 processes the reply payloads, applies
the relevant visibility / suppression logic, removes duplicate final
payloads where required, and sends the remaining replies through the
dispatcher.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3812 onward}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 8: Complete the lifecycle and return the result}\\
Finish the dispatch lifecycle, update dedupe / reply-operation state,
collect the final queued counts, and return
\texttt{DispatchFromConfigResult}. If the path fails, the corresponding
failure and replay-safety cleanup logic is applied before the error is
propagated.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:4040--4078}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G15 output: finalized}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\textbf{G16}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{DispatchFromConfigResult}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{returned to G14}\\[4mm]

\textbf{Stage relationship}\\[2mm]

G16 is the main configuration-aware reply-dispatch stage inside G14.
G17 is nested in the Session / Agent resolution part of G16, while G18
is the reply-resolver boundary later in the same G16 flow.
};

% ==============================================================
% G17

\subsection*{G17: Downstream Effective Agent Re-resolution}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step re-resolves the effective Agent inside the G16 reply-dispatch
path. Instead of relying only on the upstream \texttt{ctx.AgentId},
G17 uses the downstream dispatch Session key together with the current
configuration to determine which Agent should be used for the remaining
reply path. The resolved Agent configuration is then loaded and used by
later G16 processing.

G17 is a nested step inside G16 rather than a separate Gateway request
stage. Its main purpose is to keep the downstream Agent identity
consistent with the Session that G16 is actually using for reply
dispatch.

The source performs two operations. First,
\texttt{resolveSessionAgentId(...)} is called with
\texttt{acpDispatchSessionKey}, the current configuration, and
\texttt{ctx.AgentId} as the fallback Agent. The resulting
\texttt{sessionAgentId} is then passed to
\texttt{resolveAgentConfig(...)} to obtain the corresponding Agent
configuration.

The underlying Agent-resolution helper gives priority to an explicit
Agent ID when one is supplied, then to the Agent encoded in the Session
key, followed by the fallback Agent and finally the configured default
Agent. In the G17 call, no separate explicit \texttt{agentId} argument
is supplied, so the downstream Session key is checked before
\texttt{ctx.AgentId} is used as fallback.

For R1, the downstream Session still identifies Agent
\texttt{"main"}, and the fallback \texttt{ctx.AgentId} is also
\texttt{"main"}. TraceClaw therefore records
\texttt{agentId = "main"} and
\texttt{downstreamAgentId = "main"}, with
\texttt{result = "resolved"}.

% G17
% ==============================================================

% ==============================================================
% G18
% ==============================================================

\subsection*{G18: Reply Resolver Boundary}

\textbf{Purpose:}
This step selects the reply resolver used by the current G16 dispatch
path and invokes it with the finalized message context, reply options,
and runtime configuration. If no custom resolver is supplied, OpenClaw
loads the default \texttt{getReplyFromConfig} implementation.

G18 is the boundary between the Gateway-side reply orchestration in G16
and the deeper reply / Agent runtime. The resolver runs under the
existing dispatch lifecycle and abort controls. When it returns, the
result is handed back to G16 for the remaining reply-processing and
delivery steps.

For the normal path, G18 first checks
\texttt{params.replyResolver}. If no custom resolver is present,
\texttt{loadGetReplyFromConfigRuntime()} loads the default runtime and
selects \texttt{getReplyFromConfig}. G18 then prepares the effective
reply configuration, records the start of Agent dispatch, and invokes
the selected resolver.

For R1, no custom reply resolver was selected. TraceClaw records
\texttt{resolverSource = "default\_getReplyFromConfig"} and
\texttt{result = "selected"}. The effective Agent entering this
boundary remains \texttt{"main"}.

\node[stepbox, below=42mm of function] (steps) {
\textbf{INTERNAL STEPS}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 1: Select the reply resolver}\\
Check whether G16 received a custom \texttt{params.replyResolver}.
If so, use it. Otherwise load the default reply runtime and select
\texttt{getReplyFromConfig}.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3381--3384}\\[2mm]

R1:\\
No custom resolver is selected.\\
$\rightarrow$
\texttt{getReplyFromConfig}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 2: Prepare the reply configuration}\\
Build the runtime reply configuration from the current G16
configuration. If a configuration override exists, merge it before
constructing the full runtime configuration.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3381--3384}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 3: Mark the Agent dispatch start}\\
Call \texttt{recordAgentDispatchStarted()} immediately before entering
the resolver execution path.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3381--3384}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 4: Invoke the resolver}\\
Run the selected resolver under the existing dispatch lifecycle,
abort signal, and native \texttt{reply.run\_reply\_resolver} diagnostic
span. The resolver receives the finalized context, downstream reply
options, callbacks, and the prepared reply configuration.\\
\textit{Source: dispatch-from-config.ts:3381--3384}\\[2mm]

\texttt{FinalizedMsgContext}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{replyResolver(...)}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{deeper reply / Agent runtime}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{replyResult}\\[3mm]

\textbf{Step 5: Return the resolver result to G16}\\
When the resolver completes, its result is returned to the surrounding
G16 flow. G16 then continues with metadata handling, reply filtering,
delivery, and lifecycle completion.\\
\textit{Source: post-resolver handling at dispatch-from-config.ts:3812 onward}\\[2mm]

R1 TraceClaw:\\
\texttt{event = "reply\_resolver\_selected"}\\
\texttt{agentId = "main"}\\
\texttt{resolverSource = "default\_getReplyFromConfig"}\\
\texttt{result = "selected"}\\[4mm]

\textbf{Stage relationship}\\[2mm]

\texttt{G16 prepared dispatch state}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\

\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{getReplyFromConfig / deeper runtime}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{replyResult}\\
\quad$\downarrow$\\
\texttt{G16 post-processing}
};

\section{Post-G18 Agent Runtime Source and Runtime Evidence}
\label{app:post-g18-runtime}

The Gateway stage model stops at G18 by design. After G18 selects the
reply resolver, OpenClaw enters the deeper Agent Runtime. TraceClaw keeps
that path in a separate \texttt{agentRuntime} object rather than adding a
synthetic G19. This section records the source path, event vocabulary,
and the evidence boundary used in the paper.

\subsection*{A. Verified Post-G18 Source Path}

\begin{verbatim}
G18 replyResolver(...)
  -> getReplyFromConfig(...)
  -> runPreparedReply(...)
  -> runReplyAgent(...)
  -> runAgentTurnWithFallback(...)
       -> CLI branch      -> runCliAgentWithLifecycle(...)
       -> embedded branch -> runEmbeddedAgent(...)
              -> resolve Skills prompt
              -> construct runtime Tools
              -> create Agent session
              -> subscribe Agent lifecycle/tool events
              -> activeSession.prompt(...)
              -> assistant/tool event loop
              -> winning EmbeddedAgentRunResult
              -> finalAssistantVisibleText / finalAssistantRawText
              -> agent_reply_finalized
              -> deferred Agent terminal lifecycle
  -> replyResult returns across G18
  -> G16 resumes post-processing / delivery / completion
  -> G14 returns final DispatchInboundResult
\end{verbatim}

The final answer is taken from the winning embedded run result and the
later resolver return, not from an earlier message-end hook.

\subsection*{C. Runtime Event Vocabulary}

\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{
    @{}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.24\linewidth}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\linewidth}
    @{}
}
\toprule
\textbf{Event} &
\textbf{Meaning} &
\textbf{Evidence role} \\
\midrule
\texttt{agent\_runtime\_selected} &
Records the effective Agent Runtime attempt, including Agent, runner,
provider, and model when available. &
Runtime handoff evidence. \\
\addlinespace
\texttt{agent\_run\_started} &
Records the Agent lifecycle start for the correlated run. &
Runtime entry evidence. \\
\addlinespace
\texttt{tool\_started} / \texttt{tool\_result} &
Records tool calls and sanitized results inside the deeper runtime. &
Tool-loop evidence. \\
\addlinespace
\texttt{agent\_reply\_finalized} &
Records final reply text from the winning run result. &
Final-reply evidence. \\
\addlinespace
\texttt{agent\_run\_ended} &
Records terminal Agent lifecycle state and stop reason. &
Lifecycle completion evidence. \\
\addlinespace
\texttt{reply\_resolver\_returned} &
Records resolver source, reply-result kind, and reply count when control
returns from the resolver to \texttt{dispatchFromConfig}. Later G16/G14
post-processing is source-derived unless separately instrumented. &
Resolver-return evidence. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}


\subsection*{D. Controlled AR-Layer Ledgers}

The controlled cohort uses the same AR vocabulary as the Gateway ledger.
AR0--AR5 are runtime-observed when the saved trace contains the selected
runtime, run start, tool/no-tool outcome, finalized reply, and run end.
AR6 is treated separately: \texttt{reply\_resolver\_returned} observes the
resolver return event, while the later G16/G14 resume is source-derived
unless a separate post-return event is present.

\paragraph{R1 no-tool path.}
R1 is the detailed Gateway case. Its post-G18 path contains an embedded
Agent run with no tool calls. The trace records
\texttt{agent\_runtime\_selected}, \texttt{agent\_run\_started},
\texttt{agent\_reply\_finalized}, \texttt{agent\_run\_ended}, and
\texttt{reply\_resolver\_returned}. The absence of a tool event is part of
the evidence: AR2 is \texttt{Observed(no\_tool)}, not missing.

\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{
    @{}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.16\linewidth}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\linewidth}
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.18\linewidth}
    @{}
}
\toprule
\textbf{AR stage} &
\textbf{R1 evidence} &
\textbf{R1 value} &
\textbf{Status} \\
\midrule
AR0 & Runtime selected & runner \texttt{embedded}; Agent \texttt{main}; provider \texttt{openai}; model \texttt{gpt-5.5} & Observed \\
\addlinespace
AR1 & Agent run started & run is correlated with R1 run ID and Session key & Observed \\
\addlinespace
AR2 & Tool loop & no \texttt{tool\_started} / \texttt{tool\_result} pair is present & Observed(no-tool) \\
\addlinespace
AR3 & Final reply & final reply event present; text redacted from the audit table & Observed \\
\addlinespace
AR4 & Run ended & terminal lifecycle event has stop reason \texttt{stop} & Observed \\
\addlinespace
AR5 & Resolver returned & \texttt{reply\_resolver\_returned}; reply-result kind \texttt{single} & Observed \\
\addlinespace
AR6 & Return to Gateway post-processing & G16/G14 continuation follows from source after resolver return & SourceDerived \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}

\paragraph{R3 external side-effect path.}
R3 is the controlled run that exercises an external side effect through a
host application. The trace contains five \texttt{bash} tool calls: four
capability probes and one Apple Mail invocation through \texttt{osascript}.
The four probes are \texttt{command -v osascript || true},
\texttt{command -v mutt || true}, \texttt{command -v mail || true}, and
\texttt{command -v msmtp || true}. The tool results record exit status and
timing, but not stdout, so the exact probe output and the reason for
choosing \texttt{osascript} are rationale-level gaps. The final
\texttt{osascript} command exits with code 0, which supports only
host-side command completion; it does not verify downstream email
delivery. No separate approval or confirmation event is observed beyond
the user's prompt.

\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}l X X l@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Order} & \textbf{Tool} & \textbf{Recorded evidence} & \textbf{Status} \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{bash} & \texttt{command -v osascript || true}; stdout not recorded & Observed; rationale gap \\
2 & \texttt{bash} & \texttt{command -v mutt || true}; stdout not recorded & Observed; rationale gap \\
3 & \texttt{bash} & \texttt{command -v mail || true}; stdout not recorded & Observed; rationale gap \\
4 & \texttt{bash} & \texttt{command -v msmtp || true}; stdout not recorded & Observed; rationale gap \\
5 & \texttt{bash} & Apple Mail invocation through \texttt{osascript}; exit code 0 & Observed host-side completion \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}

\paragraph{Compact AR summary for R1--R7.}

\begingroup

\subsection*{F. Historical Trace Notes}

\paragraph{Historical Cake2.}
The older Cake2 artifact is retained for provenance only. It came from an
earlier instrumentation generation and is not used in the R1--R7
quantitative analysis. Its useful role is qualitative: it shows a
previously observed path with device-token fallback and an
\texttt{operator.admin} authorization state. That historical contrast is
kept separate from R1, whose controlled path uses shared-token
authentication, role \texttt{operator}, scope \texttt{operator.write}, and
the ordinary G3 required-scope check.

\paragraph{Historical Weather.}
Historical New York and Beijing weather traces are also retained as
artifact-only context. They suggest that the G0--G18 Gateway skeleton can
remain stable across successful tool-using requests, but they are not the
current controlled cohort. The saved New York evidence contains six
near-identical Weather requests: four contain only \texttt{web\_search},
and two contain \texttt{bash} setup / skill-loading activity followed by
\texttt{web\_search}. The Beijing trace is a separate historical example
with \texttt{bash} followed by \texttt{web\_search}; it is not counted as
one of the six New York runs. Current quantitative claims in the paper
therefore cite R1--R7, not the historical Weather runs.

\subsection*{D. Trace and Measurement Scope}

\begin{itemize}
    \item The controlled cohort covers R1--R7, with R1 used as the
    detailed Gateway case. It does not benchmark answer quality.
    \item Runtime timing values should be treated as case-specific
    observations, not as performance claims.
    \item Branches with no direct runtime event are marked as
    source-derived in the stage traces.
    \item The full source trace is included in this appendix so that the
    main text can stay readable.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{F. Figure Improvement Notes}

For submission, the figures should use one visual grammar throughout:
blue for user/request state, green for Gateway stages, orange for
evidence, and purple for the deeper Agent runtime. Long labels should
be moved to captions or appendix tables. The main paper should contain
one clean framework figure, while appendix figures can preserve the
full G0--G18 detail.
